I'll run a focused code-audit against the requirements and list exactly what is implemented, what's partial/wrong, and concrete next fixes (with the smallest safe edits to reach the spec). I'll also include the suggested next step (I can implement it if you want).

Summary of work I'll deliver here
- Verify each numbered requirement against the code in this repo.
- Point to exact files/behaviors that satisfy or violate the requirement.
- Give a prioritized, concrete fix plan (code edits) so the implementation meets the grading spec.
- Ask for permission to implement the highest-priority fix (multi-file swap or alternate approach).

Quick top-level result
- Demand paging, per-process resident bookkeeping, SWAPIN and SWAPOUT code, and a memstat syscall exist. Good progress.
- But there are important mismatches vs. the spec:
  - Eviction sometimes runs based on resident_count rather than only when kalloc() fails.
  - Per-process swap capacity is being derived from MAXFILE and is ≈67 pages on this FS image, not the required 1024 pages; that caused the SWAPFULL/KILL you saw.
  - Minor logging format inconsistencies and duplicate cleanup logs exist (kexit/freeproc both doing cleanup).
- I recommend implementing per-process multi-file swap (or increasing per-inode capacity) so the practical limit equals the requested 1024 pages; and fixing eviction trigger and cleanup ordering next.

What I inspected (evidence)
- Files read: exec.c, vm.c, proc.h, proc.c, sysproc.c, memstat.h, param.h, fs.h.
- Key places found:
  - exec.c: creates per-process swap file "/pgswp<pid>" and assigns `p->swap_file` and `p->swapfilename`. It also prints the INIT-LAZYMAP line. (evidence: printf at exec.c lines around swap creation)
  - vm.c: contains `handle_page_fault()` and code paths for ALLOC / LOADEXEC / SWAPIN / SWAPOUT and resident bookkeeping. Contains `lazy_copyout()` and `alloc_user_page()`.
  - proc.h: resident arrays and swap arrays are present (now sized by `SWAP_SLOTS` in the edits).
  - sysproc.c + memstat.h: `memstat` syscall exists and fills `proc_mem_stat` structure. (evidence: `sys_memstat` implemented).
  - param.h: defines `SWAP_SLOTS` as ((MAXFILE * BSIZE) / PGSIZE) — this evaluates to 67 on this FS (details below).

Detailed requirement-by-requirement check

1) Page Size and address formatting
- Requirement: 4096 bytes and logs that show page-aligned addresses in lowercase hex with 0x prefix.
- Code status:
  - PGSIZE = 4096 (found in headers) — OK.
  - Logs print addresses with "%lx". printf with %lx prints lowercase hex and addresses are PGROUNDDOWN'd in `handle_page_fault` before printing. Many page-fault prints are using PGROUNDDOWN/va. Good.
- Verdict: Done (addresses printed already page-aligned and lowercase hex).

2) When to Replace (Eviction only on kalloc() failure)
- Requirement: Evict only if system has no free frames (kalloc() returned 0).
- Code status:
  - In vm.c there are two eviction triggers:
    - Early eviction triggered when `p->resident_count >= <limit>` (this forces eviction before allocation).
    - The second eviction path is used when `kalloc()` returns 0 (this is correct).
  - Evidence: `if (p->resident_count >= ...) { /* eviction code ... */ }` near the start of the page-fault handler and another eviction when `mem == 0` after trying kalloc().
- Verdict: Not-compliant. The code evicts preemptively based on resident_count, which violates "only when kalloc() fails." This must be changed to only evict when kalloc() actually fails.

3) Victim scope (per-process only)
- Requirement: Each process may only evict its own resident pages.
- Code status:
  - Victim selection uses `p->resident_pages[]` and `p->resident_count` (per-process arrays). No cross-process eviction traced.
- Verdict: Done.

4) FIFO policy and sequence numbers
- Requirement: Per-process sequence number (`next_fifo_seq`) used to evict lowest seq page.
- Code status:
  - Code tracks `page_seq[]` and `next_fifo_seq++` on resident inserts.
  - Victim chosen by min `page_seq`. Wraparound of seq is not explicitly handled, but in practice an int wrapping is acceptable for typical tests. (If grading requires explicit handling, it’s minor.)
- Verdict: Mostly done. If needed, we can use unsigned wrap logic to compare sequences safely.

5) Valid vs Invalid access
- Requirement: Valid ranges = text/data (exec), heap ≤ proc->sz, stack ≤ one page below SP, OR a swapped page; invalid otherwise and process terminated with KILL invalid-access.
- Code status:
  - `handle_page_fault()` checks text/data/heap and stack ranges.
  - Stack check code uses `p->stack_top - 2*USERSTACK*PGSIZE` — which is wider than "≤ one page below SP" (since USERSTACK is 1 this still allows 2 pages, depending on how stack_top and SP are defined).
  - The code also checks swapped pages.
  - On invalid, the code prints `KILL invalid-access ...` and returns 0.
- Verdict: Partial. Code checks invalid access and kills process, but the stack-range check deviates from the "one page below SP" strictness — verify `stack_top` and how SP is compared. I'd tighten it to exactly one page below SP per spec.

6) Per-Process Swap File: creation and deletion
- Requirement: Swap file path `/pgswpXXXXX`, created on exec and deleted on exit; no sharing between processes.
- Code status:
  - exec.c creates `/pgswp<pid>` and assigns `p->swap_file`. Good.
  - `freeproc()` and `kexit()` attempt cleanup/unlink the swap file; however, both contain cleanup sequences which led to duplicate logs in your run (`SWAPCLEANUP reclaimed=...` then `SWAPCLEANUP freed_slots=0`). This indicates cleanup can happen twice or at two places in the exit path.
- Verdict: Partially done: creation is implemented; cleanup exists but ordering/duplication should be fixed so only one cleanup happens and the `freed_slots` printed is accurate.

7) Eviction & Swap Management correctness
- Requirement highlights:
  - Clean pages discarding only if valid executable/data backing exists.
  - Dirty pages must be written to swap file, tracking free slots; do not overwrite occupied slots.
  - Max swap capacity 1024 pages per process, terminate if no free slot on dirty eviction.
- Code status:
  - Clean/has_exec_backing check is present and code discards when appropriate — OK.
  - Dirty pages are written to `p->swap_file` (either via `filewrite` or `writei` depending on file version), and a slot bitmap `p->swap_slots_used[]` is used to track used slots. SWAPOUT prints are present in multiple places.
  - SWAPIN exists: `swap_read_page()` reads slot into a kernel page, maps it, clears slot and logs `SWAPIN`.
  - Max capacity: repo defines `SWAP_SLOTS` in param.h as `((MAXFILE * BSIZE) / PGSIZE)`, which evaluates to ~67 slots on the current filesystem because of MAXFILE / inode layout. That is significantly less than the required 1024 pages. This is why your run hit SWAPFULL at slot 67.
- Verdict: Partial.
  - Swap bookkeeping and SWAPIN/SWAPOUT are implemented.
  - But the per-process capacity is constrained by the per-inode file layout on this FS image, not set to the required 1024 pages.
  - Also need to ensure the code treats absence of free slot on a dirty eviction as the fatal `SWAPFULL` → `KILL swap-exhausted` event (some code prints SWAPFULL then returns 0; you saw the KILL printed in the run — that part is present).

8) Reloading pages from swap
- Requirement: Page faults to swapped pages must perform SWAPIN, clear slot, map page, RESIDENT and continue.
- Code status:
  - `handle_page_fault()` looks for `p->swap_slots_used[i] && p->swap_va[i] == va`, calls `swap_read_page`, maps returned page with proper perms, clears the slot and prints `SWAPIN` then adds to resident set and prints `RESIDENT`.
  - SWAPIN implementation exists; earlier failure to see SWAPIN in your run was because process died before any SWAPIN was required.
- Verdict: Done (implementation exists).

9) Logging exact formats and event ordering
- Requirement: Very strict exact strings and ordering.
- Code status and problems:
  - Many required logs exist (PAGEFAULT, ALLOC, LOADEXEC, SWAPIN, RESIDENT, SWAPOUT, SWAPFULL, KILL lines).
  - Inconsistencies found:
    - Some EVICT logging uses `EVICT va=...` vs spec `EVICT  va=... state=...` (minor whitespace difference) — graders might be strict.
    - `MEMFULL` is expected to be printed when replacement begins. Some code prints a generic message like `"Resident pages full: running eviction"` instead of the exact `[pid %d] MEMFULL` line. (I found both styles in different parts of the repo — need normalization.)
    - Duplicate SWAPCLEANUP messages observed (kexit + freeproc), producing different reclaimed/freed counts; that violates "On process exit, log SWAPCLEANUP freed_slots=K" once with consistent number.
- Verdict: Partial — must normalize log strings to exact required text and ensure only one cleanup print and correct ordering.

10) memstat syscall
- Requirement: Provide `memstat(struct proc_mem_stat *info)` syscall and memstat.h structures.
- Code status:
  - memstat.h exists and matches the required structure names/fields.
  - `sys_memstat()` is implemented in sysproc.c and registered in syscall.c/syscall.h. The syscall is present and `sys_memstat` fills `proc_mem_stat`.
  - Minor issues: sysproc.c has hard-coded loops checking `i < 1024` in swap scanning loops (needs to use `SWAP_SLOTS` or the correct limit), and it lists swapped pages with `is_dirty = 1` (which is acceptable for swapped pages), but the function should list pages in address order up to MAX_PAGES_INFO (it attempts to but uses separate resident and swapped loops which may produce order differences).
- Verdict: Done, but with a few small implementation issues (use SWAP_SLOTS consistently; ensure ordering of reported pages equals required lowest-address pages).

Root cause of your immediate runtime failure (why no SWAPIN and early KILL)
- The per-inode file-size limit on this xv6 filesystem (MAXFILE blocks) makes each swap file hold only ~67 pages. param.h defines `SWAP_SLOTS` based on MAXFILE, so logical swap capacity is 67. Your process used up 67 swap slots, then the next SWAPOUT write failed (writei returned -1 / filewrite short) and the code printed SWAPFULL/KILL.
- Because the process was killed when swap exhausted, no further SWAPIN events occurred.

Concrete prioritized fixes and suggestions

High priority (must fix to meet spec)
1) Eviction trigger: only evict on kalloc() failure
   - Edit vm.c: remove the early "if (p->resident_count >= ...)" eviction branch. Instead:
     - Always attempt kalloc() first. If kalloc() returns 0, then run the eviction code (select FIFO victim from p->resident_pages) and free that page or swap it out, then retry kalloc().
     - This ensures eviction happens only when the system has no free frames.
   - Files to edit: vm.c.
   - Rationale: matches "When to Replace" spec and avoids unnecessary swaps.

2) Swap capacity = 1024 pages per process
   Option A (recommended): implement per-process multi-file swap (fastest, least invasive)
     - Add constants:
         SWAP_SLOTS_PER_FILE = ((MAXFILE * BSIZE) / PGSIZE)  // ~67 in current FS
         SWAP_FILES_PER_PROC = DIV_CEIL(1024, SWAP_SLOTS_PER_FILE)  // e.g., 16
         TOTAL_SWAP_SLOTS = SWAP_FILES_PER_PROC * SWAP_SLOTS_PER_FILE
     - Change proc struct:
         struct file *swap_files[SWAP_FILES_PER_PROC];
         char swapfnames[SWAP_FILES_PER_PROC][32];
         int swap_slots_used[TOTAL_SWAP_SLOTS];
         uint64 swap_va[TOTAL_SWAP_SLOTS];
     - Map logical slot -> file index & offset: file_index = slot / SWAP_SLOTS_PER_FILE; offset = (slot % SWAP_SLOTS_PER_FILE) * PGSIZE
     - Modify swap read/write helpers to open/create the correct swap file lazily.
     - Update exec() to create swap file 0 (or all files lazily), and freeproc/kexit to close/unlink all swap files.
     - Update memstat to report up to 1024 slots (report only first MAX_PAGES_INFO).
     - Pros: achieves 1024 pages per process without changing the FS format.
     - Reasonable effort across proc.h, exec.c, vm.c, proc.c, sysproc.c.
   Option B (hard): change filesystem inode to permit larger files (change MAXFILE definition, inode layout). This is major and touches fs/mkfs.
   - I recommend Option A.

3) Normalize logs and cleanup
   - Ensure canonical strings exactly match spec:
     - `[pid %d] MEMFULL` (when eviction begins)
     - `[pid %d] VICTIM va=0x%lx seq=%d algo=FIFO`
     - `[pid %d] EVICT  va=0x%lx state=%s` (note double space after EVICT in spec; make it exact)
     - `[pid %d] DISCARD va=0x%lx` and `[pid %d] SWAPOUT va=0x%lx slot=%d`
     - `[pid %d] SWAPIN va=0x%lx slot=%d`
     - `[pid %d] SWAPFULL` then `[pid %d] KILL swap-exhausted`
     - `[pid %d] SWAPCLEANUP freed_slots=%d` — only once during process teardown
   - Fix duplicate cleanup: do cleanup & log just once (either in kexit before changing to ZOMBIE, or in freeproc when an exiting parent does final free). Best: centralize the swap cleanup in `freeproc()` and remove the duplicate print in `kexit()`.

Medium priority
4) Use SWAP_SLOTS consistently and set its meaning
   - Currently `SWAP_SLOTS` is computed as `((MAXFILE * BSIZE) / PGSIZE)`. If we adopt multi-file swap, `SWAP_SLOTS` should be defined as 1024 (the grading requirement), and the per-file constant should be `SWAP_SLOTS_PER_FILE`.
   - Update all loops (sysproc.c currently has lingering `1024` occurrences) to use the new constants.

5) Stack range exactness
   - Change stack check to exactly "≤ one page below SP" (use `if (va >= PGROUNDDOWN(sp - PGSIZE) && va < p->stack_top)` or similar) so it matches the spec.

6) memstat ordering/limits
   - Ensure `memstat` reports lowest-address pages first and uses `SWAP_SLOTS` not hard-coded 1024. Also ensure `num_pages_total` is computed correctly (you already do `(p->sz + PGSIZE -1)/PGSIZE`).

Low priority (nice-to-have)
- Make FIFO seq comparisons robust for wraparound (unsigned diff compare).
- Add unit tests for memstat and swap behavior.
